What brings you in to see me today?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 55 / personal_care_aide / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Hallucinations\nmanifestation: \"I came in because I keep hearing voices clear as somebody standing in my little studio, saying my name and telling me my work is being judged, even when the house is quiet and nobody is there.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Reserved patient.\nA reserved patient may\n1) provide brief, vague, or evasive answers to questions about their symptoms and experiences,\n2) be reluctant to answer questions about personal information, emotions, or sensitive clinical experiences, and\n3) refrain from voluntarily providing information beyond what the doctor specifically asks.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

patterns, or stand in the hallway staring like I was waiting for some signal.\n- key: S023\n  name: Negative_Symptoms\n  description: A state where emotions, motivation, speech, or social interest that should normally be present are diminished or lost.\n  manifestation: When I’m not all wound up, I can sit for hours with my coffee getting cold, not answering calls, not going to the walking club, and not even caring to pick up my paintbrush.\n- key: S033\n  name: Elevated_Irritable_Mood\n  description: A distinct period of abnormally and persistently elevated, expansive, or irritable mood.\n  manifestation: I go from feeling so bright and chosen that Greenville can’t hold all my plans to snapping hard at someone over a small thing, like a client asking me to repeat myself.\n- key: S034\n  name: Increased_Activity\n  description: Abnormally and persistently increased goal-directed activity or energy.\n  manifestation: I get so much energy that I’ll clean my whole kitchen, start three paintings, crochet half a blanket, and rewrite care schedules all in the same night without being able to sit down.\n- key: S035\n  name: Inflated_Self_Esteem\n  description: Inflated self-esteem or grandiosity (may range from uncritical self-confidence to delusional proportions).\n  manifestation: I truly believed my artwork was going to get discovered any day and that I was supposed to build a whole new care-and-art program that doctors and agencies would come learn from me.\n- key: S036\n  name: Decreased_Sleep\n  description: Decreased need for sleep (e.g., feels rested after only 3 hours of sleep).\n  manifestation: I’ve gone many nights sleeping maybe two or three hours, then getting up feeling charged like I had a full rest and going straight back to painting or making calls.\n- key: S038\n  name: Racing_Thoughts\n  description: Flight of ideas or subjective experience that thoughts are racing.\n  manifestation: My thoughts run so fast that one minute I’m thinking about a client’s medicine, then rent, then a painting title, then some message in a song, and I can’t slow it down enough to make sense.\n- key: S039\n  name: Manic_Distractibility\n  description: Attention is too easily drawn to unimportant or irrelevant external stimuli.\n  manifestation: I’ll try to finish a care note or a crochet row, but a car door outside, a bird at the window, or a color on the table pulls me away and I’m off doing something else before I know it.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: psychotic_symptoms and manic_episode occur concurrently\n  manifestation: When I was sleeping only a few hours and pushing my big art-and-care plans, I was also hearing the voices in the studio and feeling certain people were watching my work.\n- key: additional_requirement_2\n  name: Additional Requirement 2\n  description: manic_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness\n  manifestation: For most of this stretch, I was either wound up with big plans, little sleep, and nonstop activity, or coming down from that but still not back to myself.\n- key: additional_requirement_3\n  name: Additional Requirement 3\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode\n  manifestation: There was a time after my energy dropped and I was sleeping more normally when the voices and the belief that people were judging or tracking my work kept going for several weeks.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: I got to the point where my aide job was in danger because I missed shifts, mixed up care notes, and could not keep visits organized the way I used to. It sounds like they’re saying my work is being judged. Like they’re right there in the room with me, even when it’s quiet and nobody’s there.\n\nDoctor: Are the voices you hear inside your head or outside of it?\n\nPatient: Outside… it sounds like somebody’s right there in my little studio, saying my name. Not like just thoughts in my head.\n\nDoctor: Have these voices and feelings of being judged affected your daily life or ability to work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it has. I don’t really like saying it, but my aide job got shaky. The voices, and that feeling like my work’s being watched… it’s been going on since around then.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices ever tell you to do something?\n\nPatient: Not really… it’s more like they say my name, and talk like my work is being judged. Like someone’s right there, but nobody is.\n\nDoctor: Do you hear one voice or multiple voices?\n\nPatient: It’s… hard to say. Mostly it feels like one voice, clear, like somebody’s standing right there in the studio saying my name. Sometimes it’s like there’s more than one, talking about my work being judged.\n\nDoctor: Are the voices ever critical of you or your work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. It sounds like they’re saying my work is being judged. Like they’re right there in the room with me, even when it’s quiet and nobody’s there.\n\nDoctor: Are the voices you hear inside your head or outside of it?\n\nPatient: Outside… it sounds like somebody’s right there in my little studio, saying my name. Not like just thoughts in my head.\n\nDoctor: Have these voices and feelings of being judged affected your daily life or ability to work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it has. I don’t really like saying it, but my aide job got shaky. I missed some shifts, and I was mixing up care notes, not keeping the visits straight like I used to.\n\nAnd I stopped going to my walking club and those art talks at the library. Mostly I’ve just been home by myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to express your thoughts or have trouble keeping your conversations organized and on track?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe sometimes I lose my place. But I’m not sure if it’s that, or just me getting tired. I try to keep it short so I don’t get mixed up.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that your work is actually being judged or watched by someone, even when you're alone?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. I know people say it doesn’t add up, but it feels real.\n\nLike the other home-care agency, and some people from the library… I thought they were looking at my paintings and even my client papers. Like they were trying to take some chance that was supposed to be mine. Even when I’m by myself, I still feel like they’re watching.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself having trouble understanding what people are saying to you, or do you have difficulty making yourself understood by others?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean… sometimes I ask people to repeat themselves, but I don’t know if that’s what you mean.\n\nI don’t think I have a big problem making people understand me. I just try not to say too much.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like your thoughts are disjointed or hard to put into words?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not often, I guess. Sometimes I can’t find the right words, but I don’t really think about it that way. I just keep it short.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're having trouble putting your thoughts together when you're talking to someone?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. Maybe sometimes I lose what I was trying to say, but… I wouldn’t say it happens a lot. I usually just stop talking if it feels messy.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like your thoughts are disconnected from each other?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes it’s like they jump around too fast. I’ll be thinking about one thing, like a client’s medicine, and then it’s rent, then some painting name, then something I heard in a song. It gets hard to slow it down enough to make sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are disorganized or hard to follow when you're thinking to yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… inside my own head it can be hard to follow. It runs too fast sometimes—like medicine for a client, then rent, then some painting name, then a song meaning something. I don’t really… slow it down easy.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Not like just thoughts in my head.\n\nDoctor: Have these voices and feelings of being judged affected your daily life or ability to work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it has. I don’t really like saying it, but my aide job got shaky. I missed some shifts, and I was mixing up care notes, not keeping the visits straight like I used to.\n\nAnd I stopped going to my walking club and those art talks at the library. But I’m not sure if it’s that, or just me getting tired. I try to keep it short so I don’t get mixed up.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that your work is actually being judged or watched by someone, even when you're alone?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. I know people say it doesn’t add up, but it feels real.\n\nLike the other home-care agency, and some people from the library… I thought they were looking at my paintings and even my client papers. Like they were trying to take some chance that was supposed to be mine.
